People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Not like I used to, no.

Some days I’m in that small rental for hours, just sitting there, knowing there are things to do, and I can’t make myself start. I know that probably sounds… I don’t know, lazy or dramatic, but it doesn’t feel like I’m choosing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things you've done or not done?\n\nPatient: I mean… not in some big way, I don’t think. I do think about stuff I haven’t done, like not keeping up with people or letting things slide, and I don’t feel great about that.\n\nBut “guilty” sounds kind of strong. Maybe more like… embarrassed, or disappointed in myself? I’m not sure if that’s what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or sleep lately?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really kept track, honestly. I don’t want to say something’s changed if I’m not sure.\n\nSleep is… maybe not great, but I don’t know if it’s different enough to call it a change. Appetite, I haven’t noticed anything major. Sorry, is that the kind of answer you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving more slowly than usual or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: I guess… more trouble sitting still, maybe. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it was, but on patrol I started doing these things before I could really stop and think.\n\nLike I’d crouch behind counters, or signal people to get back, and then I’d catch myself pacing the same route over and over. Sometimes I’d repeat what someone said back to them, just to… check it, I guess. Staff started looking scared of me, which feels awful to say.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having thoughts that keep repeating over and over?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I think everyone gets stuck on things sometimes, right?\n\nI wouldn’t say it’s like a specific thought looping all day, at least I haven’t noticed it that way. Sometimes I go back over things in my head, but I’m not sure if that counts as “repeating thoughts.” Am I answering that the right way?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I haven’t been weighing myself regularly or anything, so I can’t give you a number, but my clothes don’t seem dramatically different.\n\nI mean, if there was a big change, I think I would’ve noticed. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Are you able to experience pleasure when you're actually doing something you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Not like I used to, no.\n\nI mean, I don’t want to sound like I’ve just given up or anything, but… I stopped hiking, I haven’t been coaching, I don’t really pick up the guitar, and I’m bad about calling people back now. Even if I think, “That used to feel good,” when I’m actually doing it—or trying to—I just feel kind of flat.\n\nSome days I’m just sitting in that small rental for hours, knowing there are things I should do, and I can’t seem to get myself started. I don’t feel like myself. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower than you used to?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed myself moving slower, exactly.\n\nMaybe I take longer to get going sometimes, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing as moving slower. Nobody’s really said anything to me about it. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so.\n\nI mean, I’ve felt pretty low and stuck, but I haven’t been sitting there making plans to hurt myself or anything like that. That’s… that’s the kind of thing you mean, right?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Yeah… I guess I do get restless sometimes.

movements or reactions inappropriate to the situation.\n  manifestation: On patrol I started reacting before I could slow myself down, like crouching behind counters, signaling people to get back, What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… I’m just not really feeling like myself lately. It’s probably not as bad as I’m making it sound, but I’ve kind of stopped doing the things I used to do—hiking, playing guitar, coaching, calling people back.\n\nSome days I’m in my rental place and I know there are things I should be doing, but I just… sit there for hours and can’t seem to get started. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it sad, exactly. Maybe… kind of blank sometimes? I mean, I’m not sitting around crying or anything. It’s more like I just don’t have much in me. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Not as much, no. I mean, I still want to say I like those things, because I do—or I used to. Hiking, playing guitar, coaching… those were kind of my normal things.\n\nLately I just don’t really get myself to do them. I’ll think about calling someone back or picking up the guitar, and then I just… don’t. Some days I’m in that little rental place for hours, knowing I should start something, and I can’t seem to make myself move on it. I don’t know if that counts as not enjoying it, or if it’s just me being stuck.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're still able to enjoy things when you actually do them, even if you have trouble getting started?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think if I actually manage to do something, maybe there’s a little bit there, but it’s hard to tell. Mostly I just don’t feel like myself anymore.\n\nI’ve stopped hiking, coaching, playing guitar, even calling people back. And it’s not like I don’t know I should. Some days I’m in that small rental for hours, just sitting there, knowing there are things to do, and I can’t make myself start. I know that probably sounds… I don’t know, lazy or dramatic, but it doesn’t feel like I’m choosing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things you've done or not done?\n\nPatient: I mean… not in some big way, I don’t think. I do think about stuff I haven’t done, like not keeping up with people or letting things slide, and I don’t feel great about that.\n\nBut “guilty” sounds kind of strong. Maybe more like… embarrassed, or disappointed in myself? I’m not sure if that’s what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or sleep lately?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really kept track, honestly. I don’t want to say something’s changed if I’m not sure.\n\nSleep is… maybe not great, but I don’t know if it’s different enough to call it a change. Appetite, I haven’t noticed anything major. Sorry, is that the kind of answer you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving more slowly than usual or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: I guess… more trouble sitting still, maybe. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it was, but on patrol I started doing these things before I could really stop and think.\n\nLike I’d crouch behind counters, or signal people to get back, and then I’d catch myself pacing the same route over and over. Sometimes I’d repeat what someone said back to them, just to… check it, I guess. Staff started looking scared of me, which feels awful to say.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having thoughts that keep repeating over and over?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I think everyone gets stuck on things sometimes, right?\n\nI wouldn’t say it’s like a specific thought looping all day, at least I haven’t noticed it that way. Sometimes I go back over things in my head, but I’m not sure if that counts as “repeating thoughts.” Am I answering that the right way?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I haven’t been weighing myself regularly or anything, so I can’t give you a number, but my clothes don’t seem dramatically different.\n\nI mean, if there was a big change, I think I would’ve noticed. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Are you able to experience pleasure when you're actually doing something you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Not like I used to, no.\n\nI mean, I don’t want to sound like I’ve just given up or anything, but… I stopped hiking, I haven’t been coaching, I don’t really pick up the guitar, and I’m bad about calling people back now. Even if I think, “That used to feel good,” when I’m actually doing it—or trying to—I just feel kind of flat.\n\nSome days I’m just sitting in that small rental for hours, knowing there are things I should do, and I can’t seem to get myself started. I don’t feel like myself. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower than you used to?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed myself moving slower, exactly.\n\nMaybe I take longer to get going sometimes, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing as moving slower. Nobody’s really said anything to me about it. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so.\n\nI mean, I’ve felt pretty low and stuck, but I haven’t been sitting there making plans to hurt myself or anything like that. That’s… that’s the kind of thing you mean, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do get restless sometimes.\n\nNot just like tapping my foot, though. On patrol, I’d start kind of… moving before I really thought it through. I’d crouch behind counters, signal people to get back, pace the same route over and over. I was trying to be careful, but I think it probably looked strange.\n\nThere were times I’d repeat what someone just said, just to make sure I’d heard it right—or, I don’t know, to check if it was part of something. Staff started looking nervous around me, and I felt bad about that. I wasn’t trying to scare anyone. Does that count as what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing significant social withdrawal, avoiding friends and family?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, maybe more than I’ve wanted to admit.\n\nI don’t want it to sound like I just don’t care about people, because I do. But I’ve stopped calling people back a lot of the time, and I don’t really go do the things I used to—hiking, coaching, playing guitar, stuff like that. I’d like to say it’s just the last few months, but that probably wouldn’t be honest.\n\nThings started kind of unraveling after I moved into the smaller rental, after my divorce. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… I’m just not really feeling like myself lately. It’s probably not as bad as I’m making it sound, but I’ve kind of stopped doing the things I used to do—hiking, playing guitar, coaching, calling people back.\n\nSome days I’m in my rental place and I know there are things I should be doing, but I just… sit there for hours and can’t seem to get started. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it sad, exactly. Maybe… kind of blank sometimes? I mean, I’m not sitting around crying or anything. It’s more like I just don’t have much in me. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Not as much, no. I mean, I still want to say I like those things, because I do—or I used to. Hiking, playing guitar, coaching… those were kind of my normal things.\n\nLately I just don’t really get myself to do them. I’ll think about calling someone back or picking up the guitar, and then I just… don’t. Some days I’m in that little rental place for hours, knowing I should start something, and I can’t seem to make myself move on it. I don’t know if that counts as not enjoying it, or if it’s just me being stuck.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're still able to enjoy things when you actually do them, even if you have trouble getting started?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think if I actually manage to do something, maybe there’s a little bit there, but it’s hard to tell. Mostly I just don’t feel like myself anymore.\n\nI’ve stopped hiking, coaching, playing guitar, even calling people back. And it’s not like I don’t know I should. Some days I’m in that small rental for hours, just sitting there, knowing there are things to do, and I can’t make myself start. I know that probably sounds… I don’t know, lazy or dramatic, but it doesn’t feel like I’m choosing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things you've done or not done?\n\nPatient: I mean… not in some big way, I don’t think. I do think about stuff I haven’t done, like not keeping up with people or letting things slide, and I don’t feel great about that.\n\nBut “guilty” sounds kind of strong. Maybe more like… embarrassed, or disappointed in myself? I’m not sure if that’s what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or sleep lately?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really kept track, honestly. I don’t want to say something’s changed if I’m not sure.\n\nSleep is… maybe not great, but I don’t know if it’s different enough to call it a change. Appetite, I haven’t noticed anything major. Sorry, is that the kind of answer you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving more slowly than usual or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: I guess… more trouble sitting still, maybe. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it was, but on patrol I started doing these things before I could really stop and think.\n\nLike I’d crouch behind counters, or signal people to get back, and then I’d catch myself pacing the same route over and over. Sometimes I’d repeat what someone said back to them, just to… check it, I guess. Staff started looking scared of me, which feels awful to say.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having thoughts that keep repeating over and over?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I think everyone gets stuck on things sometimes, right?\n\nI wouldn’t say it’s like a specific thought looping all day, at least I haven’t noticed it that way. Sometimes I go back over things in my head, but I’m not sure if that counts as “repeating thoughts.” Am I answering that the right way?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I haven’t been weighing myself regularly or anything, so I can’t give you a number, but my clothes don’t seem dramatically different.\n\nI mean, if there was a big change, I think I would’ve noticed. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Are you able to experience pleasure when you're actually doing something you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Not like I used to, no.\n\nI mean, I don’t want to sound like I’ve just given up or anything, but… I stopped hiking, I haven’t been coaching, I don’t really pick up the guitar, and I’m bad about calling people back now. Even if I think, “That used to feel good,” when I’m actually doing it—or trying to—I just feel kind of flat.\n\nSome days I’m just sitting in that small rental for hours, knowing there are things I should do, and I can’t seem to get myself started. I don’t feel like myself. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower than you used to?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed myself moving slower, exactly.\n\nMaybe I take longer to get going sometimes, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing as moving slower. Nobody’s really said anything to me about it. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so.\n\nI mean, I’ve felt pretty low and stuck, but I haven’t been sitting there making plans to hurt myself or anything like that. That’s… that’s the kind of thing you mean, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do get restless sometimes.\n\nNot just like tapping my foot, though. On patrol, I’d start kind of… moving before I really thought it through. I’d crouch behind counters, signal people to get back, pace the same route over and over. I was trying to be careful, but I think it probably looked strange.\n\nThere were times I’d repeat what someone just said, just to make sure I’d heard it right—or, I don’t know, to check if it was part of something. Staff started looking nervous around me, and I felt bad about that. I wasn’t trying to scare anyone. Does that count as what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing significant social withdrawal, avoiding friends and family?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, maybe more than I’ve wanted to admit.\n\nI don’t want it to sound like I just don’t care about people, because I do. But I’ve stopped calling people back a lot of the time, and I don’t really go do the things I used to—hiking, coaching, playing guitar, stuff like that. Mostly I just don’t feel like myself anymore.\n\nSome days I’m in that small rental for hours, and I know there are things I should do or people I should answer, but I just… can’t get myself to start. Is that the kind of withdrawal you mean?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, like you're not yourself and can't seem to get started on things?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a while. I’d like to say it’s just the last few months, but that probably wouldn’t be honest.\n\nThings started kind of unraveling after I moved into the smaller rental, after my divorce. That was about… a year and two months ago, I think. It wasn’t all at once, but since then I haven’t really felt like I got back to being me. Is that the kind of timeline you mean?\n\nDoctor: Has your current state lasted for more than two years?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think it’s been more than two years.\n\nIt’s been going on since I moved into the smaller rental after the divorce, which was about a year and two months ago. So… over a year, but not two. I hope I’m remembering that right.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your symptoms are interfering with your daily life most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I’d have to say yes, most of the time.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it’s affected work pretty badly. I got suspended from my protective service job after an incident on a routine patrol where I scared some staff and people nearby. That’s… hard to say out loud.\n\nAnd since then I’ve pulled back from a lot of things people used to count on me for—coaching, volunteering, even just being there for friends and family. I keep telling myself I’m just taking space, but… it’s probably more than that. Does that answer what you mean by interfering?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more specifically how long you've been feeling this way, is it closer to one year or more than a year and a half?\n\nPatient: It’s closer to one year than a year and a half, I think.\n\nI keep placing it around when I moved into that smaller rental after the divorce… that was about a year and two months ago. Things didn’t feel like they fell apart overnight, but that’s when they started unraveling. So, not a year and a half yet. Does that sound specific enough?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… I’m just not really feeling like myself lately. It’s probably not as bad as I’m making it sound, but I’ve kind of stopped doing the things I used to do—hiking, playing guitar, coaching, calling people back.\n\nSome days I’m in my rental place and I know there are things I should be doing, but I just… sit there for hours and can’t seem to get started. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it sad, exactly. Maybe… kind of blank sometimes? I mean, I’m not sitting around crying or anything. It’s more like I just don’t have much in me. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Not as much, no. I mean, I still want to say I like those things, because I do—or I used to. Hiking, playing guitar, coaching… those were kind of my normal things.\n\nLately I just don’t really get myself to do them. I’ll think about calling someone back or picking up the guitar, and then I just… don’t. Some days I’m in that little rental place for hours, knowing I should start something, and I can’t seem to make myself move on it. I don’t know if that counts as not enjoying it, or if it’s just me being stuck.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're still able to enjoy things when you actually do them, even if you have trouble getting started?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think if I actually manage to do something, maybe there’s a little bit there, but it’s hard to tell. Mostly I just don’t feel like myself anymore.\n\nI’ve stopped hiking, coaching, playing guitar, even calling people back. And it’s not like I don’t know I should. Some days I’m in that small rental for hours, just sitting there, knowing there are things to do, and I can’t make myself start. I know that probably sounds… I don’t know, lazy or dramatic, but it doesn’t feel like I’m choosing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things you've done or not done?\n\nPatient: I mean… not in some big way, I don’t think. I do think about stuff I haven’t done, like not keeping up with people or letting things slide, and I don’t feel great about that.\n\nBut “guilty” sounds kind of strong. Maybe more like… embarrassed, or disappointed in myself? I’m not sure if that’s what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or sleep lately?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really kept track, honestly. I don’t want to say something’s changed if I’m not sure.\n\nSleep is… maybe not great, but I don’t know if it’s different enough to call it a change. Appetite, I haven’t noticed anything major. Sorry, is that the kind of answer you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving more slowly than usual or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: I guess… more trouble sitting still, maybe. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it was, but on patrol I started doing these things before I could really stop and think.\n\nLike I’d crouch behind counters, or signal people to get back, and then I’d catch myself pacing the same route over and over. Sometimes I’d repeat what someone said back to them, just to… check it, I guess. Staff started looking scared of me, which feels awful to say.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having thoughts that keep repeating over and over?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I think everyone gets stuck on things sometimes, right?\n\nI wouldn’t say it’s like a specific thought looping all day, at least I haven’t noticed it that way. Sometimes I go back over things in my head, but I’m not sure if that counts as “repeating thoughts.” Am I answering that the right way?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I haven’t been weighing myself regularly or anything, so I can’t give you a number, but my clothes don’t seem dramatically different.\n\nI mean, if there was a big change, I think I would’ve noticed. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Are you able to experience pleasure when you're actually doing something you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Not like I used to, no.\n\nI mean, I don’t want to sound like I’ve just given up or anything, but… I stopped hiking, I haven’t been coaching, I don’t really pick up the guitar, and I’m bad about calling people back now. Even if I think, “That used to feel good,” when I’m actually doing it—or trying to—I just feel kind of flat.\n\nSome days I’m just sitting in that small rental for hours, knowing there are things I should do, and I can’t seem to get myself started. I don’t feel like myself. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower than you used to?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed myself moving slower, exactly.\n\nMaybe I take longer to get going sometimes, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing as moving slower. Nobody’s really said anything to me about it. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so.\n\nI mean, I’ve felt pretty low and stuck, but I haven’t been sitting there making plans to hurt myself or anything like that. That’s… that’s the kind of thing you mean, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do get restless sometimes.\n\nNot just like tapping my foot, though. On patrol, I’d start kind of… moving before I really thought it through. I’d crouch behind counters, signal people to get back, pace the same route over and over. I was trying to be careful, but I think it probably looked strange.\n\nThere were times I’d repeat what someone just said, just to make sure I’d heard it right—or, I don’t know, to check if it was part of something. Staff started looking nervous around me, and I felt bad about that. I wasn’t trying to scare anyone. Does that count as what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing significant social withdrawal, avoiding friends and family?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, maybe more than I’ve wanted to admit.\n\nI don’t want it to sound like I just don’t care about people, because I do. But I’ve stopped calling people back a lot of the time, and I don’t really go do the things I used to—hiking, coaching, playing guitar, stuff like that. Mostly I just don’t feel like myself anymore.\n\nSome days I’m in that small rental for hours, and I know there are things I should do or people I should answer, but I just… can’t get myself to start. Is that the kind of withdrawal you mean?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, like you're not yourself and can't seem to get started on things?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a while. I’d like to say it’s just the last few months, but that probably wouldn’t be honest.\n\nThings started kind of unraveling after I moved into the smaller rental, after my divorce. That was about… a year and two months ago, I think. It wasn’t all at once, but since then I haven’t really felt like I got back to being me. Is that the kind of timeline you mean?\n\nDoctor: Has your current state lasted for more than two years?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think it’s been more than two years.\n\nIt’s been going on since I moved into the smaller rental after the divorce, which was about a year and two months ago. So… over a year, but not two. I hope I’m remembering that right.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your symptoms are interfering with your daily life most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I’d have to say yes, most of the time.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it’s affected work pretty badly. I got suspended from my protective service job after an incident on a routine patrol where I scared some staff and people nearby. That’s… hard to say out loud.\n\nAnd since then I’ve pulled back from a lot of things people used to count on me for—coaching, volunteering, even just being there for friends and family. I keep telling myself I’m just taking space, but… it’s probably more than that. Does that answer what you mean by interfering?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more specifically how long you've been feeling this way, is it closer to one year or more than a year and a half?\n\nPatient: It’s closer to one year than a year and a half, I think.\n\nI keep placing it around when I moved into that smaller rental after the divorce… that was about a year and two months ago. Things didn’t feel like they fell apart overnight, but that’s when they started unraveling. So, not a year and a half yet. Does that sound specific enough?\n\nDoctor: Would you describe your current feelings and experiences as being consistently severe, or are there times when they are milder?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure how to rate that exactly.\n\nI’d like to say there are milder times, and I guess there are moments where I can get through things and seem okay. But overall it feels pretty heavy most days. I don’t want to overstate it, but it hasn’t really felt like something small or easy to manage.\n\nIs that the kind of answer you’re looking for?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Maybe… kind of blank sometimes? I mean, I’m not sitting around crying or anything. It’s more like I just don’t have much in me. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Not as much, no. I mean, I still want to say I like those things, because I do—or I used to. Hiking, playing guitar, coaching… those were kind of my normal things.\n\nLately I just don’t really get myself to do them. I’ll think about calling someone back or picking up the guitar, and then I just… don’t. Some days I’m in that little rental place for hours, knowing I should start something, and I can’t seem to make myself move on it. I don’t know if that counts as not enjoying it, or if it’s just me being stuck.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're still able to enjoy things when you actually do them, even if you have trouble getting started?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think if I actually manage to do something, maybe there’s a little bit there, but it’s hard to tell. Mostly I just don’t feel like myself anymore.\n\nI’ve stopped hiking, coaching, playing guitar, even calling people back. And it’s not like I don’t know I should. Some days I’m in that small rental for hours, just sitting there, knowing there are things to do, and I can’t make myself start. I know that probably sounds… I don’t know, lazy or dramatic, but it doesn’t feel like I’m choosing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things you've done or not done?\n\nPatient: I mean… not in some big way, I don’t think. I do think about stuff I haven’t done, like not keeping up with people or letting things slide, and I don’t feel great about that.\n\nBut “guilty” sounds kind of strong. Maybe more like… embarrassed, or disappointed in myself? I’m not sure if that’s what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or sleep lately?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really kept track, honestly. I don’t want to say something’s changed if I’m not sure.\n\nSleep is… maybe not great, but I don’t know if it’s different enough to call it a change. Appetite, I haven’t noticed anything major. Sorry, is that the kind of answer you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving more slowly than usual or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: I guess… more trouble sitting still, maybe. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it was, but on patrol I started doing these things before I could really stop and think.\n\nLike I’d crouch behind counters, or signal people to get back, and then I’d catch myself pacing the same route over and over. Sometimes I’d repeat what someone said back to them, just to… check it, I guess. Staff started looking scared of me, which feels awful to say.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having thoughts that keep repeating over and over?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I think everyone gets stuck on things sometimes, right?\n\nI wouldn’t say it’s like a specific thought looping all day, at least I haven’t noticed it that way. Sometimes I go back over things in my head, but I’m not sure if that counts as “repeating thoughts.” Am I answering that the right way?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I haven’t been weighing myself regularly or anything, so I can’t give you a number, but my clothes don’t seem dramatically different.\n\nI mean, if there was a big change, I think I would’ve noticed. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Are you able to experience pleasure when you're actually doing something you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Not like I used to, no.\n\nI mean, I don’t want to sound like I’ve just given up or anything, but… I stopped hiking, I haven’t been coaching, I don’t really pick up the guitar, and I’m bad about calling people back now. Even if I think, “That used to feel good,” when I’m actually doing it—or trying to—I just feel kind of flat.\n\nSome days I’m just sitting in that small rental for hours, knowing there are things I should do, and I can’t seem to get myself started. I don’t feel like myself. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower than you used to?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed myself moving slower, exactly.\n\nMaybe I take longer to get going sometimes, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing as moving slower. Nobody’s really said anything to me about it. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so.\n\nI mean, I’ve felt pretty low and stuck, but I haven’t been sitting there making plans to hurt myself or anything like that. That’s… that’s the kind of thing you mean, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do get restless sometimes.\n\nNot just like tapping my foot, though. On patrol, I’d start kind of… moving before I really thought it through. I’d crouch behind counters, signal people to get back, pace the same route over and over. I was trying to be careful, but I think it probably looked strange.\n\nThere were times I’d repeat what someone just said, just to make sure I’d heard it right—or, I don’t know, to check if it was part of something. Staff started looking nervous around me, and I felt bad about that. I wasn’t trying to scare anyone. Does that count as what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing significant social withdrawal, avoiding friends and family?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, maybe more than I’ve wanted to admit.\n\nI don’t want it to sound like I just don’t care about people, because I do. But I’ve stopped calling people back a lot of the time, and I don’t really go do the things I used to—hiking, coaching, playing guitar, stuff like that. Mostly I just don’t feel like myself anymore.\n\nSome days I’m in that small rental for hours, and I know there are things I should do or people I should answer, but I just… can’t get myself to start. Is that the kind of withdrawal you mean?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, like you're not yourself and can't seem to get started on things?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a while. I’d like to say it’s just the last few months, but that probably wouldn’t be honest.\n\nThings started kind of unraveling after I moved into the smaller rental, after my divorce. That was about… a year and two months ago, I think. It wasn’t all at once, but since then I haven’t really felt like I got back to being me. Is that the kind of timeline you mean?\n\nDoctor: Has your current state lasted for more than two years?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think it’s been more than two years.\n\nIt’s been going on since I moved into the smaller rental after the divorce, which was about a year and two months ago. So… over a year, but not two. I hope I’m remembering that right.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your symptoms are interfering with your daily life most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I’d have to say yes, most of the time.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it’s affected work pretty badly. I got suspended from my protective service job after an incident on a routine patrol where I scared some staff and people nearby. That’s… hard to say out loud.\n\nAnd since then I’ve pulled back from a lot of things people used to count on me for—coaching, volunteering, even just being there for friends and family. I keep telling myself I’m just taking space, but… it’s probably more than that. Does that answer what you mean by interfering?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more specifically how long you've been feeling this way, is it closer to one year or more than a year and a half?\n\nPatient: It’s closer to one year than a year and a half, I think.\n\nI keep placing it around when I moved into that smaller rental after the divorce… that was about a year and two months ago. Things didn’t feel like they fell apart overnight, but that’s when they started unraveling. So, not a year and a half yet. Does that sound specific enough?\n\nDoctor: Would you describe your current feelings and experiences as being consistently severe, or are there times when they are milder?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure how to rate that exactly.\n\nI’d like to say there are milder times, and I guess there are moments where I can get through things and seem okay. But overall it feels pretty heavy most days. I don’t want to overstate it, but it hasn’t really felt like something small or easy to manage.\n\nIs that the kind of answer you’re looking for?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your feelings of emptiness and lack of motivation are present every day?\n\nPatient: Pretty much every day, yeah… though I don’t know if I’d call it “emptiness” all the time.\n\nMostly I just don’t feel like myself anymore. I used to hike, coach, play guitar, call people back… and now I don’t really do those things. Some days I’m just sitting in that small rental for hours, knowing there are things I should do, and I just can’t get myself to start.\n\nI don’t mean I’m doing absolutely nothing every second, but… yes, it’s there most days. Is that what you mean?"
